SUBJECT: - SUBMISSION OF ANNUAL REPORT FOR THE FINANCIAL YEAR 2025-26 
This is to inform you that the 31st Annual General Meeting (“AGM”) of Ind-Swift Laboratories Limited will be 
held through Video Conferencing/Other Audio Visual Means (“VC/OAVM”) on Wednesday, 30th September, 
2026, at 3:00 P.M. to transact the Business, as set out in the Notice of the Annual General Meeting in compliance 
with the applicable provisions of the Companies Act, 2013 and Rules made thereunder read with relevant Circulars 
issued by the Ministry of Corporate Affairs (MCA) and Securities and Exchange Board of India (SEBI).  
In accordance with Section 108 of the Companies Act, 2013, read with Rule 20 of the Companies (Management 
and Administration) Rules, 2014, the Company has fixed Wednesday, 23rd September, 2026, as the Cut-Off Date 
to determine the entitlement of the members to cast their votes for the businesses to be transacted at the ensuing 
AGM of the Company. Other important information including a detailed procedure for remote e-voting before 
and during the AGM is provided in the notes to the AGM Notice. 
Pursuant to Section 91 of the Companies Act, 2013 and the Rules framed thereunder and Regulation 42 of SEBI 
(Listing Obligations and Disclosure Requirements) Regulations, 2015, the Register of Members and Share 
Transfer Books of the Company will remain closed from Thursday, 24th September, 2026 to Wednesday, 30th 
September, 2026 (both days inclusive) for the purpose of the 31st AGM. 
The Annual Report of the Company for the Financial Year 2025-26 along with the Notice convening the 31st 
Annual General Meeting of the Company is enclosed herewith.  
You are requested to kindly take the same on record. 
Thanking You 
For IND-SWIFT LABORATORIES LTD.

Six words. One connected 
journey. And a company whose 
best years now sit ahead of it.
Ind-Swift Laboratories 
today is leaner, clearer and 
built for a different kind of 
growth.
From Transformation to Value Creation.
Ind-Swift Laboratories is a focused, 
pure-play finished dosage formulations 
company. A single listed entity with zero 
external debt and the capacity to fund 
every phase of its growth from internal 
accruals.
The Company's strengths sit at the 
intersection of formulation science, 
regulatory precision and long-standing 
global partnerships. Its manufacturing 
facilities hold approvals from the UK 
MHRA, TGA, Health Canada and WHO 
GMP. Its R&D centre in Panchkula has 
more than 45 active programmes in the 
pipeline. Its commercial reach spans 85+ 
countries, with own-brand operations 
across the UAE, Central Asia, Africa and 
South East Asia.
TRANSFORM.  
INNOVATE.  
COMMERCIALISE.  
SCALE.  
STRENGTHEN.  
GROW.


=== PAGE 4 ===
A Company  
Rebuilt from  
First Principles
01   Transform
For nearly three decades, Ind-Swift operated 
across the pharmaceutical value chain, from active 
ingredients to finished dosages, across multiple 
entities. It was a broad business, but that breadth 
came at a cost: operational complexity, pressure 
on the balance sheet and a corporate structure 
that diluted focus.
The Reset was Decisive.
In 2024, Ind-Swift divested its API and CRAMS business 
to Synthimed Labs, backed by Bain Capital and Piramal 
through the India Resurgence Fund, in a I1,650 Crores 
transaction. Simultaneously, Ind-Swift Limited merged into 
Ind-Swift Laboratories Limited through an NCLT-approved 
consolidation, creating a single listed entity.
The Result
A unified, formulation-led company with a zero debt balance 
sheet and the freedom to invest from its own accruals.
This was more than a financial restructuring. It was a re-
architecture of the business around a clear thesis: that 
Ind-Swift's deepest competitive advantages lie in formulation 
science, regulatory expertise and long-standing global 
partnerships. Everything that followed in FY26 and everything 
that will follow in the years ahead, flows from this single, 
foundational decision.
The reset rebuilt the 
core. What comes next 
reveals its full potential.

From Ideas Today to Revenue Tomorrow

6

Where Formulation 
Science Meets 
Commercial Ambition
02   Innovate
Ind-Swift's R&D centre in Panchkula, recognised by the Department 
of Science and Technology since 1995, is where the Company's 
future pipeline takes shape. 
During FY26, the team grew from 50 to over 70 
scientists, the pipeline expanded from 30 to more 
than 45 active programmes and 15 new products 
added all targeting Europe, Australia, Canada and 
Brazil.
The direction of this work matters as much as its scale. Ind-Swift is moving beyond 
conventional oral solid dosages into complex formulation platforms: gastro-
resistant pellet systems (MUPS), delayed-release technologies and prolonged-
release tablets. 
During the year, the team developed Mirabegron Prolonged Release Tablets (25 
mg and 50 mg), a technically demanding formulation that required precision in 
release-rate engineering. It also advanced Mesalazine GR MUPS in both sachet and 
tablet formats and completed Esomeprazole GR MUPS capsules and tablets.
Six bioequivalence studies went directly to the pivotal stage, bypassing pilot 
studies entirely. That is a measure of how much confidence the team has built in 
its formulation design over the past two years.
We are well equipped to develop ointments, creams and gels, with new products 
targeted for development over the next 2–3 years. This expansion strengthens our 
product development platform and opens a second axis of growth.
Innovation is where future revenue begins. 
A deeper pipeline creates 
more pathways to filing, 
approval and market entry.

=== PAGE 6 ===
Science Reaches the 
Patient When It Reaches 
the Market
03   Commercialise
A strong pipeline has value only when it converts into regulatory 
approvals, commercial partnerships and products on pharmacy shelves. 
During FY26, Ind-Swift accelerated that conversion.
Throughout the year, the Company shared 285 dossiers with 
partners and regulatory authorities worldwide and received 69 new 
approvals across Europe, the UK, Canada, Saudi Arabia and several 
emerging markets. On the first day of patent expiry, Ind-Swift and 
its joint venture partner launched Atorvastatin and Ezetimibe in 
the European Union. This milestone demonstrated both regulatory 
precision and commercial readiness.
The most significant partnership milestone of the 
year was the progression of the Ibuprofen sachet, 
Clarithromycin dry syrup, Macrobol Sachet and 
relationship with leading MNCs to the commercial 
stage. 
Supplies across Ireland and Europe, alongside the UK and Australia, commenced during 
the current financial year (FY27), with two new products, Ibuprofen Sachet for Europe 
and Macrobol Sachet for the UK and Australia, reaching the market. This single cluster of 
partnerships is expected to contribute incremental revenue in FY27.
Beyond contract manufacturing, Ind-Swift's own-brand operations across the UAE, Africa, 
South East Asia and Central Asia, supported by over 125 medical representatives and more 
than 400 registered products, allow the Company to capture full marketing margins rather 
than the thinner economics of contract supply.
The opportunity ahead is to convert 
a growing pipeline into a wider 
commercial presence, one filing, one 
approval and one market at a time.

Existing Capacity, 
Expanded Output
04   Scale
Commercial momentum creates value when the manufacturing 
platform can keep pace.
Ind-Swift's two facilities, the Global Business Unit 
(GBU) at Derabassi in Punjab and the Samba plant 
in Jammu and Kashmir, serve distinct roles within a 
connected operating model. 
The GBU, a 100% export-oriented unit spanning approximately 81,325 square 
metres, holds approvals from the UK MHRA, TGA, Health Canada and WHO-GMP. 
It manufactures for partners including Viatris, Chanelle, Wockhardt, Teva and 
Stada and has the installed capacity to support significant revenue growth.
The Samba facility serves both domestic 
and export requirements. 
During FY26, capsule manufacturing capacity was doubled, 
ointment packing capacity grew by 150% through two new 
tube-filling lines and a new blister pack line was commissioned. 
Production volumes for the year reached approximately 39 
Crores units, averaging 115 batches per month.
The Samba facility operated at a 
partial level of its installed annual 
capacity, leaving significant scope 
to improve capacity utilisation. 
That number speaks to what lies ahead: room to grow 
volumes meaningfully from the existing asset base, with 
limited additional capital investment. As revenue scales, 
the operating leverage embedded in this cost structure will 
become visible in margins.
Growth, from here, will be driven by 
the progressive utilisation of existing 
and planned capacities expansion.

A Financial Profile that

05   Strengthen
A year ago Ind-Swift was at an inflection point with standalone negative 
operating EBITDA  of `0.40 Crores and a negative margin of 0.07% .
Through focused execution and improved operational 
efficiencies we have delivered a significant 
turnaround with EBITDA reaching `44.78 Crores by 
the end of FY26 and the operating margin improving 
to 7.04% 
This is a trajectory that reflects more than cost discipline. It reflects a 
business model that is fundamentally different from the one that 
preceded it: a higher-margin product mix, lower finance costs 
and an asset base with significant room to absorb volume 
growth.
The significance extends beyond a single period 
of performance. A stronger earnings profile 
creates the capacity to reinvest in R&D, pursue 
new market registrations and fund capacity 
upgrades, all from internal accruals, with no 
reliance on external debt.
When earnings improve at this pace, the 
entire growth conversation changes. 
Capital allocation becomes a choice, 
rather than a constraint.

A Larger Opportunity From a Wider Base
10+ 
Products Expected to Reach

Three Engines,  
One Clear Runway
06   Grow
Ind-Swift enters FY27 with three distinct revenue engines, each with its 
own margin profile, growth trajectory and strategic logic.
The first is the international CDMO and out-licensing business, anchored 
by partnerships with Viatris, Manx, Arrotex, Chanelle and Wockhardt. Contract 
manufacturing relationships here average 9 to 10 years and the CDMO business is 
targeted to grow from I180 Crores to over I600 Crores within three years. 
Upcoming launches include Clarithromycin Dry Suspension and Esomeprazole 
Capsules for Europe and Australia, each tied to named partners and defined 
timelines.
The second is the own-brand and greenfield export business, where Ind-Swift 
markets directly under its own name across the UAE, CIS, Africa and SEA countries. 
With 125+ medical representatives, 424 registered own brands and direct offices in 
Dubai and Baku these operations achieve gross margins of 50-55%, materially above 
the contract manufacturing profile.
The third is the domestic business: the Ethical division (76% gross margins, 260+ 
marketing teams reaching 25,000 doctors), the Generic division (3,000 stockists,  
14 C&F points, two verticals in HCD and Agile), the P2P contract manufacturing 
division (39 active repeat clients including Cipla, Lupin and Mankind) and the Q-DEN/
NOVA customised manufacturing vertical (advance-payment, zero credit risk).
Together, these engines provide a funded, visible pathway. The Company expects FY27 
revenue growth of more than 50%, a medium-term CAGR of 20-25% and an expansion 
of the EBITDA margin by 600 to 800 basis points over the period. 
Every initiative outlined in this report, from dossier filings and capacity upgrades to 
the Samba EU-GMP upgrade, is being funded through internal accruals. Ind-Swift will 
grow the way it has chosen to operate: from its own resources, at its own pace, on its 
own terms.

Value Creation:  
The Heart of our Next Chapter.
As We Stand Today,
Net Debt-Free,
There is a moment in the life of a company when the work of rebuilding gives way to the work of building.
For Ind-Swift, that moment arrived during FY26. The divestment is complete. The merger is behind us.  
Operating earnings, tell a story that matches the ambition.
What changes when a company reaches this point is the economics of the road ahead. Every rupee invested 
in growth, whether for a new dossier filing, capacity addition, or a field force expansion, comes from the 
Company's own accruals and cash reserves. That is a position of considerable strategic freedom.
A clean balance sheet gives growth a stable foundation. When financial flexibility meets a business with 
expanding opportunities, the arithmetic of value creation begins to compound.
We find ourselves at a new starting line.  
A clear foundation. A wide opportunity.
Ind-Swift Laboratories retains a 7.80% equity stake in Synthimed Labs, the entity that now houses its former API and CRAMS 
business. Synthimed Labs Pvt Ltd, a portfolio company of India Resurgence Fund is currently valued at I8,000-9,000 Crores. 
The Company continues to participate in the growth of the business it built over decades, even as it focuses entirely on the 
formulations future it has chosen.
The chapter ahead is a purposeful stride towards 
accelerated value creation.  
And that journey is already underway.

CHAIRMAN’S STATEMENT
Dear Shareholders,
I write to you at a moment that feels qualitatively different from anything in our recent past. After several 
years of restructuring, divestment and consolidation, Ind-Swift Laboratories has arrived at a point of 
genuine clarity. We know what we are. We know what we do well. And we have the financial strength, the 
operational platform and the market positioning to make the years ahead count.
FY26 was the year in which that clarity took definitive shape. Through the completion of our merger with 
Ind-Swift Limited, the full integration of our formulations businesses and a sustained focus on operational 
discipline, we have emerged as a unified, pure-play finished-dosage formulations company. We are net 
debt-free and for the first time in several years, the numbers tell a story that matches the intent.
What the Numbers Say
On consolidated basis revenue from operations for FY26 
rose to I641.29 Crores, a 13% increase over FY25. More 
importantly, the trajectory within the year tells a story 
of its own. Q4 FY26 revenue stood at I169.79 Crores, 
up 22.82% year-on-year and nearly 10% sequentially. 
Profit After Tax for the quarter (Q4 FY26) reached I14.93 
Crores, a 39% improvement over Q3 FY26. Profit before 
exceptional items nearly tripled during the year to 
approximately I61 Crores.
These are early returns and I present them with the 
sobriety they deserve. But they are real, directionally 
consistent and built on a business model that is 
structurally sounder than at any point in our history. The 
balance sheet tells the same story.
Following the I1,650 Crores divestment of our API and 
CRAMS business to Synthimed Labs, backed by Bain 
Capital and Piramal through the India Resurgence Fund, 
we have repaid every rupee of external debt. We carry 
no borrowing cost to speak of. The capital we deploy for 
growth comes from our own accruals and cash reserves. 
That is a position of considerable strategic freedom.
It is worth noting that the promoter group has backed 
this strategic direction with its own capital. In February 
2026, Essix Biosciences Limited converted fully 
convertible warrants into 51 Lakhs equity shares at 
I121 per share, investing approximately I61.71 Crores 
and increasing the promoter group’s holding from 
approximately 39.5% to 43%. When promoters invest at 
this scale into a company they already control, it reflects 
a considered judgement about the value that lies ahead. 
It is a freedom and a conviction that we have put to 
productive use on the shop floor.
Building from the Shop Floor Up
The financial reset would mean little without operational 
progress and I am particularly proud of what our teams 
accomplished on the ground during FY26. At our Samba 
facility in Jammu & Kashmir, capsule manufacturing 
capacity was doubled from 1 Crores to 2 Crores units per 
month. Ointment packing capacity was scaled up from 
0.50 Crores to 1.25 Crores units per month by adding 
two new tube-filling lines. A new blister pack line was 
commissioned for our oral solid-dosage packaging. 
Production volumes for the year reached approximately 
39 Crores units.
What gives these volumes their real weight is the quality 
architecture behind them. Not a single domestic product 
recorded a non-standard quality event during the 
year. We cleared GMP audits from Cipla, JB Chemicals, 
Mankind and Lupin, as well as a regulatory audit 
from Uganda. Our facilities meet EU-GMP compliance 
standards and, during the year, secured updated ISO 
14001:2015 and ISO 45001:2018 certifications for 
environmental management and occupational health 
and safety. We also received an EcoVadis Silver Medal for 
sustainability performance.
Taken together, these credentials represent a quality and 
compliance infrastructure that meets the most exacting 
global standards and they are earned one audit, one 
batch and one decision at a time.
Equally significant is the growth headroom that this 
operational base now offers. Capacity utilisation at the 
Samba plant stands at approximately 40%. That number 
matters for what it implies: we can materially increase 
volumes from our existing asset base with minimal 
incremental capital investment.

=== PAGE 12 ===
When revenue doubles, the operating leverage embedded 
in our cost structure will become clearly visible in our 
margins. And the market in which we operate gives us 
every reason to believe that this volume growth will come.
The Market We Serve
I want to spend a moment on the broader context, 
because it frames the scale of the opportunity before us. 
India’s pharmaceutical exports crossed US$ 30 billions for 
the first time in FY25, growing at a CAGR of 7% over the 
last decade. Formulations and biologicals account for 75% 
of that export base. The domestic pharmaceutical market 
stands at approximately US$ 60 billions and is projected to 
reach US$ 130 billions by 2030. The Indian Pharmaceutical 
Market recorded 10.3% value growth as recently as April 
2026, with chronic therapy segments such as cardiac and 
anti-diabetic leading the momentum.
Behind this growth sits a structural advantage that 
continues to widen. India supplies 20% of the world’s 
generic medicines by volume, manufacturing over 60,000 
generic brands across 60 therapeutic categories. Patent 
expiries on blockbuster molecules between 2024 and 2026 
have opened a US$ 4 billions annual window for Indian 
generic manufacturers and the Government’s Production-
Linked Incentive scheme continues to draw fresh 
investment into domestic formulation and API capacity.
Ind-Swift Laboratories, as a pure-play formulations 
exporter with a presence in over 85 countries, is positioned 
squarely to benefit from these tailwinds. The question is 
no longer whether the opportunity exists. The question is 
how effectively we capture it. And on that front, we have a 
plan built on specifics rather than aspirations.
The company is also evaluating joint venture (JV) 
opportunities for local production in select international 
markets, with the aim of expanding its marketing 
reach and unlocking additional growth opportunities. 
Discussions are already underway in markets such as 
Malaysia, Vietnam, Senegal, Saudi Arabia and Australia.
The company intends to pursue these opportunities in 
a phased manner, focusing on establishing one JV at a 
time before progressing to other identified markets. This 
step-by-step approach is expected to enable the company 
to build capabilities and experience while selectively 
pursuing further opportunities.
Clear Visibility on Growth
The most immediate catalyst is our partnership with 
Viatris, under which commercial supplies commenced 
during Q1 FY27. This single relationship is expected to 
contribute an incremental I200–220 Crores of revenue in 
FY27. Alongside this, we have upcoming product launches 
across Europe, the UK and Australia in partnership with 
leading pharma MNCs spanning Ibuprofen Sachet, 
Clarithromycin Dry Suspension, Macrogol Sachet and 
Esomeprazole Capsules. These are commercial realities 
with defined timelines and they are the reason I speak of 
visibility rather than ambition.
Beyond contract manufacturing, our Own-Brand and 
Greenfield operations offer a different and in many ways 
more valuable kind of opportunity. We have deployed over 
125 medical representatives across the UAE, Uzbekistan 
and Central Asia, supporting more than 400 registered 
products, with plans to expand further into East Africa. 
These markets allow us to capture full marketing margins 
rather than the thinner economics of pure contract 
supply and they represent some of the highest-return 
opportunities in our portfolio.
The domestic business adds a third layer of growth with its 
own distinct margin profile. Our Ethical division achieves 
a gross margin of 76%, supported by a network of over 
3,000 stockists and 14 C&F points. Chronic therapies in 
cardiac, anti-diabetic and CNS segments, precisely the 
categories recording double-digit growth across the 
Indian pharmaceutical market, are where our domestic 
portfolio is concentrated.
Taken together, these three engines give us a clear 
pathway. Our CDMO business is targeted to triple over 
four years. Our overall ambition is to double revenue from 
our current base of approximately I640 Crores to over 
I1,200 Crores by FY29, at a topline CAGR of 20–25%. As 
our revenue mix tilts further towards Domestic Ethical 
products and Export Own Brands, which carry superior 
gross margins, we expect Operating EBITDA margins to 
expand by 600 to 800 basis points.
To build the intellectual property foundation for this 
growth, we intend to file over 400 new dossiers by FY27. 
All of this- capacity debottlenecking, regulatory capex and 
Greenfield expansion- is being funded through internal 
accruals. We will grow as we have chosen to operate: 
without debt, without dilution of discipline and without 
shortcuts.
A Word of Thanks
I extend my sincere appreciation to our Board of Directors 
for their guidance through a year of significant strategic 
decisions. I thank our regulatory and institutional 
partners for their continued trust. And I thank you, our 
shareholders, for the confidence you have placed in this 
organisation through a period of big structural change.
I also want to acknowledge the people who make this 
company run. It is significant to me that during FY26, the 
Company allotted shares under its employee stock option 
programme. This step aligns our team's interests with our 
shareholders' long-term interests when the people who 
build a company choose to own a stake in it; that tells you 
something about the conviction within its walls.
The hard work of restructuring is behind us. The 
integration is complete. The balance sheet is clean. The 
market opportunity is large and growing. And the team 
that runs this company, a team that now has skin in the 
game alongside you, walks into FY27 with a sense of 
purpose and a plan that is fully funded.
Ind-Swift Laboratories is a company whose best years lie 
ahead. I look forward to demonstrating that, quarter by 
quarter, as we build something worthy of the trust you 
have placed in it.
With warm regards,
N.R. Munjal
Executive Chairman

STATEMENT BY THE MANAGING DIRECTOR, GLOBAL OPERATIONS
today, we hold 866 active product 
registrations globally, supported by 
424 marketing authorisations. Each 
registration represents a product, a 
relationship with a regulator, a customer 
and a market and this is a foundation 
we intend to build on systematically.
Expanding across Africa & 
Central Asia
Africa has been a particularly 
encouraging story. During the year, we 
expanded both our marketing presence 
and our product basket across the 
continent, strengthening our position 
in a market that offers considerable 
long-term potential. Central Asia, too, 
remains an emerging growth corridor 
and our strategic entry into this region 
marks a step toward building a more 
diversified international portfolio 
through own brand building. We see 
sustained opportunity in both regions 
and are committed to building deeper 
roots. 
Reaching out to Latin  
America
Latin America which until now was 
relatively a less explored territory for us 
is now the focus area for next financial 
year.  Recognizing the immense 
potential that lies in this territory which 
includes markets such as Brazil, Mexico, 
Peru, Chile and others, the organization 
has already finalised an aggressive 
expansion plan which shall yield high 
returns for the company. The company 
has lined up more than 50 dossier 
filings within the next one year in these 
markets in alliance with strong local 
partners. 
Winning Large-scale Tenders
The year brought considerable volatility 
across global markets. Rising input costs 
placed sustained pressure on margins, 
while geopolitical developments—from 
the prolonged Russia-Ukraine conflict to 
disruptions along critical trade routes—
added further complexity to an already 
strained global supply chain. Our 
response was measured and deliberate. 
We managed costs while maintaining 
the quality and delivery timelines our 
customers expect and stayed close to 
our markets, adjusting in real time to 
shifting conditions. Importantly, we 
won several significant tenders across 
different continents during the year, 
which delivered strong volumes and 
validated our approach to market 
engagement.
Delivering on Our 
Commitments
The bottom line is this: despite the 
turbulence, we met our budgeted 
targets for FY26. The tenders we 
secured, the new geographies we 
entered and the discipline we brought 
to cost management all came together 
to deliver on the targets we had set. It 
tells us that our approach to building 
this business is working and that 
customers continue to place their 
confidence in us.
Strengthening our R&D 
Pipeline
The breadth of our market access, 
however, is only as strong as the 
products behind it. This is why we have 
invested in transforming our product 
development engine. Previously, we 
introduced approximately 7–8 new 
molecules annually. Today, our pipeline 
has expanded to 30+ molecules per 
year, a step change in our ability to 
serve diverse markets and customer 
needs.
What makes this expansion particularly 
promising is the nature of the 
molecules themselves. These are niche, 
highly complex formulations with 
strong growth potential and limited 
competition. This combination of 
technical differentiation and favourable 
market dynamics positions us for 
healthy returns over the coming years 
and allows us to continually refresh 
our product basket with more relevant 
offerings.
Dear Shareholders,
FY26 was a year of steady progress for 
our global operations. The international 
environment remained challenging, marked 
by elevated raw material costs, geopolitical 
uncertainty and supply-chain disruptions. 
Through it all, we stayed focused on what 
matters most: serving our customers reliably 
and strengthening the foundations of our 
international business. Allow me to take you 
through the progress we have made and the 
direction we are heading in.
Entering the Ointment Space
Building on this momentum, we are 
also looking to create a presence in the 
ointment space—a niche, high-capex 
delivery platform where specialised 
manufacturing capabilities create 
meaningful barriers to entry. With more 
than 20+ products in the pipeline, 
this area  represents another avenue 
for value creation, one that is well 
aligned with our strategy of pursuing 
differentiated, hard-to-replicate product 
categories. 
Looking Ahead
As our product range widens and 
volumes grow, manufacturing readiness 
becomes the next priority. In FY27, 
we plan to scale up our capabilities 
by investing in process automation, 
enabling us to improve throughput, 
maintain consistent quality at higher 
volumes and strengthen our cost 
competitiveness as we take our 
products into more markets.
Encouragingly, our tender pipeline for 
FY27 appears strong, with a healthy 
line-up of opportunities across our 
key geographies. Combined with 
the regulatory approvals we have 
accumulated, the new product 
introductions from our expanded R&D 
engine and our growing presence in 
regulated markets, we are confident 
that we will meet our budget targets for 
FY27 as well.
A Note to Our Shareholders
The pieces are coming into place: a 
wider geographical footprint, a stronger 
regulatory base, a deeper and more 
differentiated product portfolio and 
manufacturing capabilities that are 
evolving to match. The road ahead 
is defined by purposeful expansion. 
Our commitment to you is simple: 
disciplined execution, sustainable 
growth and long-term value creation.
Warm regards,
Sahil Munjal
Managing Director, Global Operations

STATEMENT BY THE MANAGING DIRECTOR, DOMESTIC  OPERATIONS
Realigning Distribution for 
Deeper Penetration
With this base in place, our priority 
during the year was to make it more 
productive. We initiated a Direct-
to-Stockist model, supported by 
our nationwide C&F network, with 
a straightforward objective: reduce 
distribution layers, improve product 
availability at the point of sale and 
generate higher volumes from 
our existing stockist base. We are 
complementing this with market-wise 
mapping and digital tools to identify 
underserved geographies and sharpen 
our coverage where it matters most.
Expanding and Sharpening 
the Product Portfolio
Alongside distribution, we moved to 
widen our product portfolio. Our Agile 
Division, launched with around 150 
specialty-focused products, expanded 
to around 200 products during the year, 
adding depth to this relatively new 
vertical. The established HCD network 
continues to provide the backbone for 
broader distribution of our branded-
generics business.
More importantly, we are deliberately 
pivoting toward higher-margin, 
prescription-led therapies. Chronic 
categories, particularly cardiovascular, 
diabetes and CNS, represent the next 
wave of expansion for our domestic 
business. Our ethical formulations 
carry materially higher margins than 
purely volume-led generics, making this 
segment strategically more valuable as 
a proportion of our portfolio. Building 
our presence in this segment improves 
both the pace and the quality of our 
earnings.
Engaging Customers and 
Prescribers
Distribution and product breadth 
deliver results when credible on-the-
ground relationships support them. 
During the year, we deepened our 
engagement with doctors through 
knowledge-based engagements, wider 
coverage and quality in-chamber time 
with the prescribing community. We 
also expanded our presence through 
professional associations and clinical 
seminars, building the kind of credibility 
that drives prescription behaviour over 
time. Our objective goes beyond adding 
retail touchpoints: we are focused on 
improving productivity, increasing 
conversion and building lasting 
relationships across the value chain.
Investing in Manufacturing 
Capability
A wider product range and expanding 
market reach must be backed by 
manufacturing capability that matches 
thier ambition. In February 2026, we 
approved an investment of around 
I40 Crores to upgrade our Samba 
facility to EU-GMP and PIC/S standards. 
While this investment is primarily 
directed at opening regulated export 
markets, the improvements in utilities, 
process controls, data integrity and 
quality systems will also improve the 
reliability and scalability of our domestic 
manufacturing operations.
The Road Ahead: FY27 and 
Beyond
Looking ahead, we are targeting 
domestic business growth of 
approximately 15–20% annually, 
supported by four strategic priorities.
First, we intend to extend our presence 
into peninsular India, extending the 
Ethical Division across the southern

Domestic Operations
A Consolidated Platform with National Reach
The integration has made the true scale of our domestic 
presence visible. Today, we reach more than 25,000 doctors

Rooted in science.  
Guided by purpose.  
Built for enduring trust.
About the Company
Ind-Swift, a globally trusted pharmaceutical company 
founded in 1995 in Chandigarh, has evolved over 
nearly three decades into a research-led finished 
dosage formulations company with a strong domestic 
and international presence.
The Company manufactures a wide range of 
formulations, including tablets, capsules, sachets, dry 
syrups, ointments and pellets, spanning therapeutic 
areas such as cardiovascular care, diabetology, 
gastroenterology, gynaecology and paediatrics.
Product development pipeline spans a diverse 
range of therapeutic areas, including anti-diabetic, 
cardiovascular, anti-hypertensive, antiprotozoal, 
antifungal and antibiotic therapies, as well as 
treatments for urinary bladder syndrome, hepatic 
encephalopathy, IBS-D, ulcerative colitis, Crohn’s 
disease and acid reflux/GERD, including proton pump 
inhibitors (PPIs).
Its manufacturing facilities, accredited by the UK 
MHRA, TGA, Health Canada and WHO-GMP, operate 
to the highest international quality standards. Its R&D 
centre in Panchkula, recognised by the Department 
of Science and Technology, Government of India, 
since 1995, is equipped with advanced analytical 
infrastructure and a team of over 70 scientists focused 
on complex formulation development, process 
validation and regulatory submissions.
Today, Ind-Swift is a single, unified listed entity, 
operating as a pure-play finished dosage formulations 
company with a net debt-free balance sheet and a 
clear strategic focus on what it does best: developing, 
manufacturing and delivering quality medicines to 
patients across 85+ countries.

LARGEST REVENUE-GENERATING REGION
A well-established presence across the UK, Europe, Canada, Australia and South Africa, supported by approvals from 
the UK MHRA, TGA, Health Canada and WHO-GMP, keeps this region central to the Company’s growth strategy.
FY26 Highlights: Successful Day-One launch of the Atorvastatin-Ezetimibe combination in Europe with a leading 
generic pharmaceutical partner. Validation batches and regulatory submissions completed for two products at 
the newly commissioned manufacturing facility. Long-standing collaborations with Chanelle, Tiefenbacher, Viatris, 
Polpharma, Amarox, Stada, Egis and Arrotex across co-development, contract manufacturing and out-licensing.
Opportunity Ahead: High generic adoption, favourable reimbursement policies and healthcare cost-containment 
initiatives continue to support market growth. Europe alone accounts for 47% of the Company’s export revenue.
Our Five Strategic Markets
01 | Europe and Highly Regulated Markets
BUILDING REGIONAL DEPTH
Direct operations in Dubai, Baku and Dushanbe, supported 
by technical offices focused on pharmacovigilance and 
regulatory compliance, provide a strong platform for 
regional expansion.
Strategic partnerships in Saudi Arabia and Iraq have 
delivered business growth during the year. Market 
acceptance in Tajikistan and Azerbaijan continues to 
develop and registrations in Georgia and Armenia are 
expected to support commercial operations in the coming 
year.
In the UAE, the Company maintains a direct field force 
comprising a Regional Head, NSM, SM and 6 Medical 
Representatives, supported by a growing portfolio of 50 
product registrations. In Saudi Arabia, the Company is 
pursuing multiple business models for the region’s largest 
market, with 8 product registrations in place and 13 more 
expected by 2027, targeting annual sales of US$ 5 millions.
In Qatar (11 SKUs registered), Oman (25 SKUs registered) 
and Bahrain (3 SKUs registered), the Company operates 
through a distributor model, with further products in the 
registration pipeline.
Opportunity Ahead: Pharmaceutical localisation, 
centralised procurement and healthcare modernisation 
are expanding demand for cost-effective medicines and 
domestic manufacturing partnerships.
02 | Middle East and CIS
A FAST-GROWING STRATEGIC MARKET
Africa remains one of the Company’s fastest-growing 
and most strategically important regions. Established 
operations in Abidjan, Dar es Salaam, Kampala and Nairobi, 
supported by dedicated marketing and regulatory teams, 
provide deep local access and market engagement.
Plans include expansion into Rwanda, Zambia, Zimbabwe 
and Togo, alongside deeper collaboration with Tender 
Authorities to improve access to essential and emergency 
medicines. The Company has also been awarded a supply 
tender for the Francophone region.
Opportunity Ahead: High reliance on imported medicines 
and limited domestic manufacturing continue to create 
demand for reliable international suppliers.
03 | Africa
A CONSISTENT GROWTH PLATFORM
South East Asia continues to deliver momentum. The 
Company’s established presence across Malaysia, 
Singapore, Hong Kong, Thailand and the Philippines 
reflects growing product acceptance across institutional 
tenders and private healthcare markets.
Opportunity Ahead: Expanding universal healthcare 
coverage and government procurement programmes 
are creating opportunities for affordable generic 
medicines across the region.
04 | Asia-Pacific
AN EMERGING GROWTH FRONTIER
Strategic alliances and an expanding product 
registration base are building the Company’s presence 
across Central and South America.
FY26 Expansion: Expanded presence across Chile, 
Colombia and Peru. Secured additional product 
registrations to support future market entry. Plans to 
establish a dedicated branding team in Peru to deepen 
market engagement. Ten product dossiers are planned 
for submission in Brazil, with both GBU and Jammu 
facilities preparing for ANVISA inspection.
Opportunity Ahead: Expanding public healthcare 
programmes, growing retail pharmacy networks and 
evolving regulatory frameworks continue to support 
adoption of generic medicines.

Across the  
Indian Market
Strengthening Our Ethical Business
The Ethical business delivered strong progress during FY26, 
supported by focused execution, portfolio strengthening 
and continued investments in brand development. Despite a 
challenging healthcare environment, the segment recorded 
21% growth over the previous year, led by the strong 
performance of its flagship Anin Group.
The newly launched Matravi brand also established a 
promising market presence, crossing ₹3 Crores in IQVIA 
sales. Its early performance reflects encouraging market 
acceptance and demonstrates the Company’s growing 
capabilities in identifying market opportunities and 
developing differentiated brands.
The business also recorded 50% growth in YPM, contributing 
to a significant improvement in overall business performance 
and supporting the segment’s turnaround. Going forward, 
the focus will remain on converting this momentum into 
sustainable growth by strengthening established brands, 
developing new brands and expanding market penetration.
The Company is further strengthening its Paediatric portfolio, 
with a strategic focus on Probiotics and Vitamin D3. These 
segments offer significant market potential and are expected 
to provide additional avenues for portfolio expansion. 
Alongside its existing growth engines, the Company is 
targeting an overall business milestone of ₹50 Crores, with a 
YPM objective of 2+ Lakhs across its operating markets.
The growth strategy is centred on consolidating the 
Company’s legacy brands while creating new growth 
platforms through focused brand development. The 
Company is also evaluating opportunities to expand its 
portfolio and strengthen its presence across relevant 
therapeutic segments.
Going forward, the Ethical business will maintain a sharp 
focus on business development and brand building, 
supported by scientific marketing initiatives, stronger 
prescription generation and deeper engagement with 
healthcare professionals. The approach will focus on 
translating scientific value propositions into stronger 
brand recall, prescription adoption and sustained market 
performance.
The segment’s key priorities remain Quality, Innovation, 
Brand Strength, Market Expansion and Sustainable Growth. 
Continued investments in people, products, technology and 
strategic partnerships will further strengthen the capabilities 
of the Ethical business and support its transition towards a 
more agile, innovation-led growth model.
With a balanced focus on legacy brand consolidation, new 
product development, scientific marketing and market 
expansion, the Ethical segment is well positioned to build 
on its current momentum and create sustainable growth 
opportunities in the years ahead.
Our Generics Leadership
Ind-Swift Laboratories Limited has been one of the 
early players in India’s branded generics market and has 
maintained a strong presence in the segment for nearly 
three decades. With a portfolio of over 700 products 
across a wide range of therapeutic categories, the 
Healthcare Division has built a trusted reputation for 
providing quality medicines to patients across India.
To strengthen its market position and create new growth 
opportunities, the Company is making its distribution 
model more efficient and responsive. It has adopted 
a Direct-to-Stockist (DTS) approach through its CFA 
network across India, supported by a Direct-to-Retailer 
(DTR) model. The DTR model will be supported by the 
upcoming SwiftPlus app, a digital platform designed to 
create a direct connection with stockists and retailers. 
These initiatives are expected to improve market 
reach, increase business volumes and strengthen the 
Healthcare Division’s presence across the country.
The Company Successfully Driving the Agile Division, a 
new generics vertical with a specialty-focused portfolio 
of around 150 products, supported by a dedicated 
distribution channel. The division is being actively scaled 
to expand its market presence. Building on this progress, 
the Company plans to launch another generics division 
in FY 2026–27, with approximately 200 additional 
products, including pharmaceutical molecules newly 
introduced in the Indian market.
Through these initiatives, the Ind-Swift Group is 
expanding its generics business by broadening its 
product portfolio, expanding its distribution network 
and reaching more markets. The Company remains 
focused on building on its strong foundation and 
creating the next phase of growth in the branded 
generics segment.
Supporting Verticals
NOVA
The NOVA division offers a diversified portfolio of multi-
specialty pharmaceutical products, manufactured strictly 
in line with customer-specific requirements. Products 
are supplied under advance payment arrangements, 
supporting efficient working capital management, 
robust cash flows and minimal credit risk.
P2P (PEER-TO-PEER / CONTRACT 
MANUFACTURING)
The P2P division specialises in loan licensing and 
contract manufacturing for several reputed names in 
the pharmaceutical industry. Its key clients include Dr. 
Reddy’s Laboratories, Sun Pharma (Ranbaxy), Micro Labs, 
Abbott, Sandoz, Novartis, Lupin, Cipla and Unichem. The 
division enables the Company to deploy its advanced 
manufacturing capabilities while reinforcing long-term 
strategic partnerships with leading pharmaceutical 
companies.
Domestically, we operate across multiple business verticals, 
each focused on addressing diverse market needs and 
customer segments. This diversified approach supports 
strong and resilient revenue streams while drawing on our 
expertise across both traditional and modern medicine. 
The division is expanding its presence across peninsular 
India and preparing to enter high-potential therapeutic 
segments, including cardiovascular, diabetology and anti-
infectives, creating new avenues for long-term growth and 
consolidating our market position. These initiatives are 
expected to further expand Ind-Swift’s generics portfolio, 
enhance its market presence and reinforce its longstanding 
leadership in the branded generics segment.

international regulatory approvals, advanced production technologies and flexible capacity to manufacture diverse dosage

Global Business Unit
Engineered for International Markets
Derabassi, Punjab
The GBU at Jawaharpur, Derabassi, is a 100% export-oriented 
facility spanning approximately 81,325 square metres, 
designed to meet the requirements of the world’s most 
demanding regulatory authorities, including the UK MHRA 
and Health Canada.
Advanced automation, integrated production systems 
and rigorous quality frameworks support the full journey 
from formulation development to large-scale commercial 
manufacturing. The facility’s capabilities span high-volume 
products and specialised formulations, giving the Company 
the flexibility to respond to evolving partner requirements 
and market opportunities.
A Trusted Global Manufacturing Partner
The GBU works with leading pharmaceutical companies 
including Teva, Stada, Chanelle, Synthon, Viatris and 
Wockhardt across contract manufacturing, co-development, 
dossier out-licensing and Day-One generic launches.
From Development to Market: A Connected 
Manufacturing Pathway
DEVELOP: R&D trial batches establish formulation feasibility.
VALIDATE: GMP-compliant exhibit and validation batches 
support regulatory and performance requirements.
TRANSFER: Technology transfer moves products efficiently 
into commercial manufacturing.
SCALE: Optimised processes enable consistent, high-volume 
production.
COMMERCIALISE: Products reach the market with regulatory 
and quality requirements embedded throughout.
GBU Technology and Production
The GBU combines advanced production systems with adaptable manufacturing lines that support both high-volume 
commercial products and specialised formulations. 
Together, these systems strengthen product integrity, traceability and supply-chain authentication.

The facility manufactures tablets, capsules, dry syrups

and utilities.

3015 blister packing machine for Alu-Alu packaging.
Quality and compliance: Expanded bulk quarantine and 
warehouse areas. Dedicated tablet tooling and packaging 
change-part rooms. Raw material drum storage areas. Two 
HPLC systems, a UV spectrophotometer, an FTIR, a pH meter, 
an incubator and a Milli-Q water system.
Engineering and utilities: Replacement of ageing air 
compressors. New refrigerated air dryer. Chiller efficiency 
improvements. Reduced steam and water losses. Energy 
conservation initiatives.
The facility operated at approximately 40% of its annual 
capacity during FY26. The investments completed during the 
year have created room for meaningfully higher production 
volumes as demand scales.

02 | Scaling Advanced Technology Platforms
During FY26, the Company advanced its capabilities in 
Gastro-Resistant (GR), Delayed-Release (DR) and Multi-Unit 
Pellet System (MUPS) technologies, enabling the development 
of formulations with superior pharmacokinetic profiles, 
improved patient compliance and enhanced therapeutic 
performance.
Formulation milestones achieved during FY26: 
Esomeprazole GR MUPS in both capsule and tablet dosage 
forms. Mirabegron Prolonged Release Tablets (25 mg and 50 
mg), a technically demanding formulation requiring precision 
in release-rate engineering. Pantoprazole Gastro-Resistant 
Tablets, Rabeprazole Gastro-Resistant Tablets, Mesalazine GR 
MUPS in both sachet and tablet formats.
A key milestone during the year was the commissioning of a 
semisolid technology laboratory equipped with integrated 
formulation and analytical capabilities for ointments, 
creams and gels. This facility opens a second axis of product 
development alongside the oral solid dosage portfolio, with 
more than 22 semisolid formulations planned over the next 24 
to 36 months.
03 | Investment in R&D Infrastructure
During FY26, the Company invested in enhancing its R&D 
infrastructure by upgrading its laboratories and adding 
analytical instruments to support formulation development, 
method validation and regulatory compliance. 
Key additions included: Four High-Performance Liquid 
Chromatography (HPLC) systems. One Gas Chromatography 
Headspace (GC Headspace) system. One UV-Visible 
Spectrophotometer. 
Research and Development
A Foundation of  
Scientific Excellence
At Ind-Swift, Research and Development is where future growth takes shape. The Company’s DST-recognised 
R&D Centre in Panchkula serves as the hub of formulation innovation, process development and regulatory 
preparation, covering the full cycle from concept development and formulation design through analytical 
validation, bioequivalence studies, technology transfer and market readiness.
During FY26, the Company accelerated its transition from conventional oral solid-dosage formulations to specialised delivery 
platforms and complex pharmaceutical technologies. By expanding capabilities in advanced formulation systems, investing 
in scientific infrastructure and focusing entirely on products for the UK, Europe, Australia, Canada and Brazil, the Company is 
building a pipeline of high-value products designed to deliver durable growth and long-term competitive advantage.

05 | Intellectual Property
Intellectual property is integral to the 
Company’s innovation approach. Beyond 
conventional patent filing, Ind-Swift follows 
a Freedom-to-Operate (FTO) framework to 
develop formulations that can enter global 
markets with reduced litigation risk. During 
FY26, the Company filed six patents, primarily 
focused on innovative manufacturing 
processes. This approach enables the 
Company to design around innovator patents, 
accelerate market entry following patent 
expiry and build proprietary products with 
durable market positioning.

Platform

the successful execution of 14 technology 
transfer projects during FY26, of which three 
products have been registered and launched 
commercially, demonstrating the Company’s 
ability to move products seamlessly from the 
laboratory to the manufacturing floor.

Ind-Swift’s, responsibility extends beyond the products it manufactures. The Company invests in the 
communities around its operations and in the environmental commons they share. Our philosophy of 
“Caring Beyond Business” translates into focused initiatives across ecological restoration, education, disaster 
relief and rural employability.
During the year, the Company directed its efforts towards four areas where its resources could create tangible, lasting 
outcomes.  
Relief and Support for Flood-Affected Communities in Punjab
When floods struck parts of Punjab during the year, the Company extended financial assistance for immediate 
relief and recovery.
Corporate Social Responsibility  
& People Development
Restoring Green Cover through Tree Plantation
As part of our commitment to environmental 
sustainability, the Company undertook a Tree Plantation 
Drive in partnership with the Citizen Welfare Association, 
Sector-25, Panchkula, at Village Jhuriwala, Panchkula. The 
initiative focused on an area that was previously used as a 
dumping ground and it is now being redeveloped into a 
green forest zone for ecological restoration. the Company 
sponsored the plantation of over 1,000 trees, contributing 
to increased green cover, improved environmental 
conditions and enhanced biodiversity.
The event was graced by the Chairman of the Ind-Swift 
Group as Chief Guest, who personally participated in the 
plantation activity, underscoring the leadership’s personal 
involvement in the Company’s environmental agenda.
Impact: A neglected site is being restored as a permanent 
ecological asset for the community.
Empowering Education through Technology
The Company continued to invest in educational 
infrastructure for government schools near its operations.
Our Focus Areas:
Digital Learning Support: The Company donated a 
Samsung LCD to the Government Elementary School, 
Jawaharpur, Derabassi, supporting the integration of 
digital tools into classroom learning.
Strengthening Infrastructure: The contribution improved 
the school’s teaching infrastructure and supported a more 
interactive classroom environment.
Supporting the Community: The initiative reflects the 
Company’s focus on strengthening learning outcomes in 
communities adjacent to its facilities.
Creating Lasting Impact: A small investment in digital 
tools is creating a measurably better learning environment 
for young students.
Creating Livelihoods through Skill Development
Rural youth near the Company’s facilities often have the 
aptitude for industrial work but limited access to structured 
training. The Company sees this as both a responsibility and a 
practical opportunity.
Under the National Apprenticeship Promotion Scheme (NAPS), 
the Company provided industry-based training to over 120 
unemployed rural youth, enabling them to gain practical 
exposure within a professional industrial environment.
	
The apprentices received hands-on exposure to 
production, quality control, packaging and other key 
functions, helping them translate theoretical knowledge 
into practical, workplace-ready skills.
	
The Company supported these apprentices by providing 
stipends entirely from Company resources, demonstrating 
its dedication to youth development even in the absence 
of an immediate manpower requirement.
	
The initiative was designed not merely as a training 
intervention, but as a pathway towards employability, self-
reliance and sustainable livelihoods for rural youth.
Impact: The initiative empowered 120+ rural youth with 
practical industry skills, workplace confidence and enhanced 
employability. For many participants and their families, 
it represented an important first step towards formal 
employment and financial independence, contributing to a 
sustainable cycle of skill development, livelihood creation and 
community empowerment.
Contribution to Flood-Affected Families 
The contribution of  ₹10 Lakhs provided valuable support to flood-affected 
families and reaffirmed the Company’s commitment to community welfare, 
compassion and responsible corporate citizenship. Through this initiative, the 
Company demonstrated that reinforcing its philosophy of “Caring Beyond 
Business.” 
Our Way Forward
In FY27, the Company will expand its rural skilling programmes, invest further in healthcare access near its facilities and continue 
its ecological restoration work. The goal is not breadth of activity but depth of outcomes.

Comprehensive Security & Benefits
The Company provides a structured benefits architecture 
covering financial protection, healthcare, retirement 
security and educational support. Each programme is 
designed to address a specific stage of an employee’s 
career and family life.
	
Group Term Insurance: Coverage of I35 Lakhs for 
every employee, providing financial protection to 
families in times of need. Through this policy, the 
Company has supported 20+ employee families.
	
Group Medical Insurance: Health coverage of up to 
I5 Lakhs, helping employees manage healthcare needs 
with greater confidence. The policy has supported 
400+ employees.
	
Parents’ Medical Cover: A dedicated medical 
insurance policy for employees’ parents, offering 
cashless treatment or reimbursement at network 
hospitals, over and above the employee’s own Group 
Medical Cover.
	
Preventive Healthcare: Suraksha Chakra: Employees 
are eligible for reimbursement of actual preventive 
health check-up expenses once every financial year. In 
addition, the Company organises twice-yearly health 
camps through empanelled laboratories, with the cost 
borne by the Company.
	
Group Gratuity Policy: Coverage for all employees 
from the date of joining, providing future-service 
gratuity benefits up to the age of 58 years to the 
family in the event of an employee’s death during 
service. Over the past five years, 22+ gratuity-linked life 
insurance cases have been fully settled for employee 
families.
	
Retirement Planning: Benefits through the 
Superannuation Fund and National Pension System 
(NPS) support employees in building financial security 
for a dignified post-retirement life.
	
Protsahan Scholarship Programme: An employee 
welfare initiative supporting the educational 
aspirations of employees and their dependent children 
through financial assistance. The programme operates 
across three levels , Protsahan I, II and III to support 
deserving individuals in achieving their educational 
goals.

The Company’s people practices have been recognised across national and international platforms. These accolades reflect a 
consistent approach to employee welfare, workplace culture and talent development.

retain experienced talent and give every team member a reason to stay and build a career.
Our Way Forward

needs. The objective is to build an organisation where operational ambition and people development move

=== PAGE 29 ===
The global economy remained resilient in 2025 despite 
macroeconomic volatility, geopolitical tensions, trade 
disruptions and fiscal pressures. Global GDP growth stood at 
3.3% during the year, supported by steady performance across 
advanced economies and continued growth momentum in 
emerging markets.
Major Economies in 2025: Advanced economies recorded 
moderate growth supported by technology investments, 
stable labour markets and resilient consumer spending. The 
United States witnessed gradual improvement driven by fiscal 
support and consumer resilience, while Europe continued to 
recover gradually amid stable domestic demand. Emerging 
markets continued to lead global growth, supported by 
domestic consumption, manufacturing activity, infrastructure 
investments and expanding access to essential goods and 
services.
Inflation and Commodity Trends: Global inflation moderated 
during the year and is expected to ease further in 2026. 
Commodity prices remained mixed, while oil prices stayed 
relatively stable due to improved supply conditions. However, 
inflationary risks remained sensitive to geopolitical tensions, 
trade disruptions and energy-market volatility.
Trade Landscape: Increased tariff actions and evolving 
trade policies created uncertainty across global markets. 
Nevertheless, global trade remained resilient, supported 
by merchandise trade, supply-chain realignment and 
diversification of sourcing networks. These developments 
reinforced the importance of resilient supply chains, 
diversified markets and cost-efficient manufacturing 
platforms.
The United Kingdom
Steady Growth Amid Global Uncertainty
The United Kingdom economy is expected to maintain a 
moderate growth trajectory in FY2025–26, supported by 
policy measures to strengthen economic stability, moderate 
inflation and encourage investment. GDP growth stood at 
1.5% in 2025, up from 1.1% the previous year, reflecting a 
gradual improvement in economic conditions and resilient 
domestic demand.
Inflationary pressures eased during the year, enabling 
policymakers to adopt a more balanced monetary policy 
stance. The Bank of England reduced the repo rate to 3.75% 
by the end of the year, with lower borrowing costs expected 
to support consumer spending, enhance business confidence 
and stimulate investment activity across sectors.
However, the long-term outlook remains constrained by weak 
productivity growth, geopolitical uncertainties and ongoing 
global conflicts, which continue to pose structural challenges 
to the UK economy.
Asia-Pacific
Stability with Momentum
The Asia-Pacific region maintained steady economic 
momentum in FY2025–26, supported by resilient domestic 
demand, improving trade activity, infrastructure investments 
and easing inflationary pressures across several economies. 
The region continued to remain a major contributor to global 
economic growth, led by strong performances from India, 
China, Southeast Asia, Japan and Australia.
According to the Asian Development Bank (ADB), developing 
Asia recorded growth of 5.1%, supported by strong domestic 
consumption and improving exports. Inflation across 
the region moderated to 1.6%, reflecting stable financial 
conditions along with easing food and energy prices in several 
economies.
The region continued to witness growth in manufacturing, 
technology, semiconductors, electric vehicles, renewable 
energy and digital infrastructure, strengthening its position 
as a global manufacturing and innovation hub. Supply chain 
diversification and rising foreign direct investment inflows 
also supported industrial expansion and trade activity across 
emerging Asian economies.
Central banks across the region gradually adopted more 
balanced monetary policy approaches as inflationary 
pressures eased, with stable financial conditions and lower 
interest rates expected to support consumer spending, 
business confidence and investment activity.
However, the regional outlook remains challenged by rising 
tariff measures, global trade uncertainties, geopolitical 
tensions, including the US-Iran conflict, high energy prices 
and slower growth in key global markets, which may continue 
to affect exports, commodity prices and overall investment 
sentiment.
LATAM Region
A Gradual Recovery
Latin America and the Caribbean witnessed moderate 
economic growth in FY2025–26, reflecting gradual 
improvement in trade activity, easing external pressures and 
relatively stable market conditions. According to the Economic 
Commission for Latin America and the Caribbean (ECLAC), the 
region’s GDP is expected to expand by 2.4% during the year.
South America is likely to benefit from stronger trade with 
China and improving commodity prices, while Central America 
and the Caribbean continue to record steady economic 
activity. However, the region’s long-term outlook remains 
constrained by weak productivity growth, limited fiscal 
flexibility, slowing formal employment generation and global 
trade uncertainties. Rising geopolitical tensions, inflationary 
pressures and financial market volatility may continue to 
affect investment sentiment, exports and overall economic 
momentum across the region.
MENA Region
Resilience Amid Transformation and Uncertainty
The Middle East and North Africa (MENA) region continued to 
demonstrate resilience, growing by 4.0% in 2025, supported 
by economic diversification initiatives, infrastructure 
investments and the expansion of non-oil sectors. While 
geopolitical tensions and global economic uncertainty posed 
challenges, several economies across the region benefited 
from strong government spending, tourism growth, digital 
transformation and private-sector development.
Growth across the region remained uneven. The Gulf 
Cooperation Council (GCC) economies continued to 
outperform many peers, witnessed a growth of 3.2% in 2025, 
driven by robust non-oil activity and the gradual easing 
of oil production constraints. Saudi Arabia, the UAE, Qatar 
and Oman witnessed sustained momentum in sectors such 
as tourism, logistics, financial services, manufacturing and 
technology, reflecting the success of long-term diversification 
strategies. GCC growth is expected to strengthen further in 
2026, supported by higher hydrocarbon output and continued 
expansion of non-oil industries.
Digital transformation emerged as a key economic 
driver across the region. Governments and businesses 
accelerated investments in artificial intelligence, fintech, 
smart infrastructure and digital public services, enhancing 
productivity and supporting the growth of new economic

However, the regional outlook remains influenced by

Ongoing conflicts and disruptions to key trade and energy

Outlook
The global economic outlook remains cautiously positive, with 
growth expected to remain steady across major economies. 
However, geopolitical tensions, tariff actions, supply chain 
disruptions and financial market volatility may continue to 
influence business conditions. For pharmaceutical companies, 
this environment highlights the importance of market 
diversification, regulatory readiness, supply-chain resilience 
and cost-efficient manufacturing capabilities.
India remained one of the fastest-growing major economies 
during FY26, supported by steady consumption, improving 
rural demand, resilient services activity and continued 
manufacturing momentum. Easing inflation, stable financial 
conditions and improving household confidence supported 
demand across essential and discretionary categories, 
including healthcare and pharmaceutical products.
Macroeconomic Environment
Inflation moderated during the year, supported by stable 
commodity prices, improved supply conditions and favourable 
food-price trends. With inflationary pressures easing, the 
Reserve Bank of India adopted a more supportive monetary 
stance, helping improve liquidity and credit conditions across 
the economy.
Improved employment conditions, rising incomes, 
urbanisation and expanding healthcare access continued 
to strengthen consumption demand. These trends 
supported growth across branded, generic and value-added 
pharmaceutical products, particularly in chronic therapies and 
essential medicines.
Industrial Landscape
India’s industrial and manufacturing activity remained 
resilient, supported by domestic demand, export 
opportunities, formalisation and government policy support 
for manufacturing-led growth. Strong GST collections, 
improving infrastructure and rising participation in global 
supply chains continued to reflect the broadening of India’s 
economic base.

=== PAGE 31 ===
The global pharmaceutical sector continued to chart a strong 
growth trajectory in 2025, shaped by rising healthcare needs, 
accelerating medical innovation and expanding access to 
advanced therapies. The global Pharmaceuticals market 
stood at US$ 1.7 trillions in 2025, growing at a CAGR of 6.08%. 
Therapeutic segments such as oncology, immunology, 
diabetes and obesity continued to redefine the industry 
landscape, with obesity therapies emerging as one of the 
fastest-growing categories worldwide.
The pharmaceutical industry continues to evolve through 
rapid advancements in biologics, biosimilars, antibody-
drug conjugates (ADCs), cell therapies and AI-enabled drug 
discovery, accelerating innovation and reshaping treatment 
landscapes. As companies strengthen their pipelines through 
strategic collaborations and global licensing partnerships, 
demand for efficient, scalable manufacturing solutions is 
rising in parallel.
In this environment, finished dosage formulations—including 
tablets, capsules, injectables, liquids and topical products—
remain a critical component of the pharmaceutical value 
chain, supported by expanding healthcare access, rising 
prevalence of chronic diseases, ageing populations and 
increasing demand for affordable medicines. At the same 
time, the growing reliance on contract manufacturing and 
development partners is creating significant opportunities for 
formulation-focused CMO/CDMO players.
Asia Pacific
The Asia Pacific pharmaceutical market generated revenues of 
US$ 355.5 billions in 2025 and it is expected to grow at a CAGR 
of 7.1% from 2026-2033. Conventional drugs (small molecules) 
accounted for the largest share of market revenue in 2025, 
while biologics and biosimilars (large molecules) are expected 
to emerge as the fastest-growing segment during the 
forecast period. The region contributed 20.5% of the global 
pharmaceutical market revenue in 2025 and it is anticipated 
to remain the fastest-growing regional market, reaching 
approximately US$ 611.7 billions by 2033.
Supporting this rapid pharmaceutical expansion, the Asia 
Pacific pharmaceutical contract manufacturing (CMO/CDMO) 
market is also witnessing strong momentum, driven by rising 
outsourcing activity, expanding biologics manufacturing 
and increasing demand for cost-efficient pharmaceutical 
production solutions. The market was valued at US$ 74.04 
billions in 2025 and it is poised to reach approximately US$ 
156.91 billions by 2035, growing at a CAGR of 7.80% during 
2026–2035.
The region has emerged as a key global manufacturing 
hub, supported by strong API production capabilities, 
improving regulatory standards, expanding pharmaceutical 
infrastructure and rising investments in complex therapies 
such as biologics, biosimilars and sterile injectables.
The United Kingdom
The UK pharmaceutical market was valued at US$ 44.1 billions 
in 2025 and it is expected to reach US$ 75.2 billions by 2034, 
growing at a CAGR of 5.93% during 2026–2034. Market 
growth is primarily driven by the rising prevalence of chronic 
diseases, increasing advancements in personalised medicine 
and bioengineering.
In particular, Bioengineering and personalised medicine are 
advancing rapidly in the UK, reshaping healthcare delivery 
through genetic-profile-based treatment approaches. These 
innovations are improving treatment precision, enhancing 
efficacy and reducing side effects, marking a strong shift 
toward precision medicine for complex diseases such as 
cancer and autoimmune disorders.
Supporting this evolving therapeutic landscape, the UK 
pharmaceutical contract manufacturing market generated 
US$ 6,726.7 millions in revenue in 2025 and is expected to 
reach US$ 14,478.7 millions by 2033, expanding at a CAGR 
of 10.2% over the period. API manufacturing remained the 
largest revenue-generating segment in 2025.
As a major European hub for outsourced pharmaceutical 
manufacturing, the UK benefits from strong R&D capabilities 
and a robust regulatory framework. The CMO ecosystem spans 
small molecules, biologics, vaccines and sterile injectables, 
reflecting the increasing complexity and specialisation of 
modern drug development.
LATAM Region
The Latin American pharmaceutical market was valued at US$ 
135.98 billions in 2025 and it is poised to grow at a CAGR of 
7.03% during 2026–2034.
This growth is driven by the region’s expanding healthcare 
needs and its evolving disease landscape. The pharmaceutical 
sector spans prescription medicines, generic drugs, over-the-
counter (OTC) products and biopharmaceuticals, with key 
markets including Brazil, Mexico, Argentina, Colombia, Chile 
and Peru. Serving as a critical component of the healthcare 
ecosystem, the industry continues to support both public 
health systems and private healthcare providers in addressing 
a wide spectrum of medical needs.
A major catalyst for this expansion is the rising burden of 
chronic and non-communicable diseases (NCDs), which 
account for over 70% of deaths in the region, according to the 
WHO. Conditions such as diabetes, cardiovascular diseases, 
cancer and respiratory disorders are increasingly prevalent, 
intensifying the demand for continuous and advanced 
treatment options. For instance, in Mexico, cardiovascular 
diseases alone account for nearly 30% of annual deaths.
Further reinforcing this trend is the region’s demographic shift. 
With a rapidly ageing population expected to exceed 100 
millions people aged 60 and above by 2030, the need for long-
term therapies, speciality drugs and geriatric care solutions is 
rising steadily, thereby strengthening the long-term growth 
outlook for the Latin American pharmaceutical market.
MENA Region
The Middle East pharmaceutical market was valued at US$ 
57.05 billions in 2025 and is projected to reach US$ 78.30 
billions by 2033, expanding at a CAGR of 4.04% during 
2025–2033. This steady growth is primarily driven by the rising 
prevalence of chronic diseases, an ageing population and 
increasing healthcare expenditure across the region.
At the same time, the market is undergoing a structural 
transformation supported by strategic healthcare reforms, a 
growing emphasis on local pharmaceutical manufacturing 
and the integration of advanced technologies. Governments 
across the region are actively strengthening regulatory 
frameworks and encouraging domestic production, 
particularly in high-value segments such as biologics and 
vaccines, to reduce dependence on imports and enhance 
healthcare self-sufficiency.
A significant growth driver is the increasing burden of 
chronic diseases. The EMRO region records some of the 
highest diabetes prevalence rates globally, ranging from 7% 
in Somalia to 18% in Egypt. Additionally, countries such as 
Bahrain, Kuwait, Saudi Arabia and the United Arab Emirates 
report obesity and overweight rates exceeding 70%, especially 
among women, while physical inactivity remains widespread. 
These factors are contributing to a rising demand for long-
term therapeutic solutions, including advanced lipid-lowering 
and metabolic treatments.
Alongside epidemiological pressures, regional governments 
are fostering collaborations with global pharmaceutical 
companies to build local production capabilities. Furthermore, 
technological advancements and evolving regulatory 
standards are reshaping the pharmaceutical landscape, 
enabling improved efficiency, innovation and market access. 
Collectively, these developments highlight the Middle East’s 
transition toward a more self-reliant and innovation-driven 
pharmaceutical ecosystem.

India is one of the world’s leading producers of generic 
medicines, supplying over 20% of global generic drug 
demand by volume and manufacturing nearly 60,000 
generic brands across around 60 therapeutic categories. The 
pharmaceutical ecosystem spans an integrated value chain — 
API manufacturers, formulation companies, contract research 
organisations and distribution networks — with large-scale 
production capabilities across oral solids, injectables, biologics 
and speciality therapies.
The Indian pharmaceutical market generated revenues of US$ 
68.38 billions in FY2025-26, expanding at a 10.98% CAGR. The 
formulation market grew around 6.5% over the same period, 
supported by rising healthcare awareness, expanding access 
and strong demand for affordable medicines. The increasing 
prevalence of chronic diseases — diabetes, cardiovascular 
disorders, cancer — is accelerating demand for advanced drug 
formulations. While conventional formulations continue to 
account for the largest market share, biologics, biosimilars and 
speciality injectables are emerging as key growth segments.
Improvements in healthcare infrastructure, regulatory 
frameworks, manufacturing technologies and R&D capabilities 
continue to strengthen India’s competitiveness in global 
supply chains. At the same time, the industry navigates 
challenges including pricing pressures, rising U.S. tariffs and 
supply chain disruptions.
Export Market: India's pharmaceutical exports rose over 2% 
year-on-year to US$ 31.11 billions in FY26, demonstrating 
resilience despite a sharp decline in March amid the fallout 
from the West Asia conflict. Europe has emerged as a key 
growth region for pharmaceutical and drug formulation 
exports, with the ongoing India–EU and India–UK free trade 
agreement discussions expected to improve market access, 
strengthen regulatory cooperation and ease non-tariff 
barriers.
CMO/CDMO: The Indian CMO market is estimated at US$ 
25.81 billions in FY2025-26 and is poised to reach US$ 
57.94 billions by 2031 at a CAGR of 14.43%. Rising global 
outsourcing of biologics and complex injectables, coupled 
with investments in greenfield facilities and AI-enabled 
manufacturing, is reinforcing India’s position as a preferred 
outsourcing destination. Stricter regulatory standards are 
simultaneously strengthening quality systems and enhancing 
export credibility.
Outlook
The outlook for the global pharmaceutical industry remains 
strong, with rising healthcare demand, increasing prevalence 
of chronic diseases, ageing populations and continuous 
advancements in drug development shaping the sector’s 
growth trajectory. Growing adoption of biologics, biosimilars, 
precision medicine and speciality therapies continues to 
transform the industry landscape, while sustained investments 
in research and development are accelerating innovation 
across major markets. The market is poised to reach US$ 2.78 
trillions by 2033, expanding at a CAGR of 6.08% during the 
period.

Demand Catalysts
India–UK CETA (July 2025): Provides zero-duty access for 56 
pharmaceutical tariff lines, improving the competitiveness 
of Indian generics in the UK — India’s largest pharma export 
market in Europe.
Rising Chronic Disease Burden: The growing prevalence of 
diabetes, cardiovascular diseases, women’s health conditions 
and lifestyle-related disorders is supporting sustained demand 
for long-term therapies and finished dosage formulations.
PLI Schemes: Have already helped avoid imports worth I3,591 
Crores of APIs, KSMs and drug intermediates, strengthening 
manufacturing resilience and self-reliance in critical inputs.
Bulk Drug Parks: Three parks approved in Andhra Pradesh, 
Gujarat and Himachal Pradesh with a total project cost 
exceeding I6,306.68 Crores, including I3,000 Crores in 
central assistance, to strengthen domestic API manufacturing 
capacity.
PMBJP: Over 18,646 Jan Aushadhi Kendras are operational as 
of March 2026, offering 2,110 medicines, 315 medical devices 
and consumables across 29 therapeutic segments.
Biopharma SHAKTI (Budget 2026–27): Outlay of I10,000 
Crores over five years to position India as a global 
biopharmaceutical hub, focusing on biologics and biosimilars 
capabilities.
Governance and Compliance: CDSCO oversees drug 
approvals and licensing, NPPA regulates pricing and the 
Indian Pharmacopoeia Commission sets quality standards — 
together ensuring robust oversight and strengthening global 
confidence in Indian pharmaceuticals.
Outlook
India’s pharmaceutical formulations sector is well-positioned 
for sustained growth, supported by strong domestic 
demand, expanding export opportunities and a competitive 
manufacturing base. Continued investments in R&D, biologics 
and complex formulations — along with PLI support and 
trade agreements — are expected to enhance global 
competitiveness further. With rising outsourcing to India’s 
CMO/CDMO ecosystem and improving healthcare access, the 
market is expected to reach US$ 174.67 billions by 2034.

Business and Financial Overview 
FY 2025-26
Financial Performance (Consolidated Basis)
FY26 was the first full year of the reconstituted Ind-Swift 
Laboratories. Following the NCLT-approved merger of 
Ind-Swift Limited (effective August 8, 2025), the Company’s 
financials now reflect the combined entity, accounted for 
under the pooling of interest method as prescribed by Ind AS 
103.
Year-on-year comparisons should be read with this context 
in mind, as the prior-year base reflects only ISLL's pre-merger 
operations.
On a consolidated basis, revenue from operations rose to 
₹641.29 Crores, a 13% increase over ₹574.24 Crores in FY25. 
This growth was broad-based. Export revenues contributed 
₹493.76 Crores, led by the Punjab 100% Export-Oriented Unit 
(EOU), which serves regulated and semi-regulated markets in 
85+ countries.
